<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="115.41px"/><col width="498.76px"/><tr data-conditional="_i2c2_v2" height="100.00px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_i2c2_v2" style="display: none"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 31</p></td><td data-conditional="_i2c2_v2" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">STOPFACLR</span>: STOP detection flag (STOPF) automatic clear</p><p class="TableIndent">0: STOPF flag is set by hardware, cleared by software by setting STOPCF bit.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: STOPF flag remains cleared by hardware. This mode can be used in NOSTRETCH target mode, to avoid the overrun error if the STOPF flag is not cleared before next data transmission. This allows a target data management by DMA only, without any interrupt from peripheral.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_i2c2_v2" height="85.33px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_i2c2_v2" style="display: none"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 30</p></td><td data-conditional="_i2c2_v2" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ADDRACLR</span>: Address match flag (ADDR) automatic clear</p><p class="TableIndent">0: ADDR flag is set by hardware, cleared by software by setting ADDRCF bit.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: ADDR flag remains cleared by hardware. This mode can be used in target mode, to avoid the ADDR clock stretching if the <span data-keyref="~i2c2_IPACRONYM">I2C</span> enables only one target address. This allows a target data management by DMA only, without any interrupt from peripheral.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_i2c2_v2" height="22.67px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_i2c2_v2" style="display: none"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 29:<span data-conditional="_i2c2_v2,_noi2c2_smb3">25</span></p></td><td data-conditional="_i2c2_v2" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_noi2c2_v2" height="22.67px"><td data-conditional="_noi2c2_v2"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:24</p></td><td data-conditional="_noi2c2_v2"><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_i2c2_smb3" height="116.00px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_i2c2_smb3" style="display: none"></td><td data-conditional="_i2c2_smb3" style="display: none"></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_i2c2_v2" height="56.00px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_i2c2_v2" style="display: none"></td><td data-conditional="_i2c2_v2" style="display: none"></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_noi2c2_smbus" height="22.67px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_noi2c2_smbus" style="display: none"></td><td data-conditional="_noi2c2_smbus" style="display: none"></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus" height="56.00px"><td data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 23</p></td><td data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus"><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">PECEN:</span> PEC enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: PEC calculation disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: PEC calculation enabled</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus" height="130.67px"><td data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 22</p></td><td data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ALERTEN</span>: SMBus alert enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: The SMBALERT# signal on SMBA pin is not supported in host mode (SMBHEN = 1). In device mode (SMBHEN = 0), the SMBA pin is released and the alert response address header is disabled (0001100x followed by NACK). <br/>1: The SMBALERT# signal on SMBA pin is supported in host mode (SMBHEN = 1). In device mode (SMBHEN = 0), the SMBA pin is driven low and the alert response address header is enabled (0001100x followed by ACK).</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>When ALERTEN = 0, the SMBA pin can be used as a standard GPIO.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus" height="54.67px"><td data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 21</p></td><td data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">SMBDEN</span>: SMBus device default address enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Device default address disabled. Address 0b1100001x is NACKed.<br/>1: Device default address enabled. Address 0b1100001x is ACKed.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus" height="54.67px"><td data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 20</p></td><td data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">SMBHEN</span>: SMBus host address enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Host address disabled. Address 0b0001000x is NACKed.<br/>1: Host address enabled. Address 0b0001000x is ACKed.</p></td></tr><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 19</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">GCEN</span>: General call enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: General call disabled. Address 0b00000000 is NACKed.<br/>1: General call enabled. Address 0b00000000 is ACKed.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_i2c2_wkp,_i2c2_wkpautonomous" height="72.00px"><td data-conditional="_i2c2_wkp,_i2c2_wkpautonomous"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 18</p></td><td data-conditional="_i2c2_wkp,_i2c2_wkpautonomous"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">WUPEN</span>: Wake-up from Stop mode enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Wake-up from Stop mode disabled.<br/>1: Wake-up from Stop mode enabled.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>WUPEN can be set only when DNF[3:0] = 0000.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_noi2c2_wkp" height="22.67px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_noi2c2_wkp" style="display: none"></td><td data-conditional="_noi2c2_wkp" style="display: none"></td></tr><tr height="102.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 17</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">NOSTRETCH</span>: Clock stretching disable </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is used to disable clock stretching in target mode. It must be kept cleared in controller mode.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Clock stretching enabled<br/>1: Clock stretching disabled</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit can be programmed only when the <span data-keyref="~i2c2_IPACRONYM">I2C</span> peripheral is disabled (PE = 0).</p></td></tr><tr height="70.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">SBC</span>: Target byte control</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is used to enable hardware byte control in target mode. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: Target byte control disabled<br/>1: Target byte control enabled</p></td></tr><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 15</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RXDMAEN</span>: DMA reception requests enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: DMA mode disabled for reception<br/>1: DMA mode enabled for reception</p></td></tr><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 14</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TXDMAEN</span>: DMA transmission requests enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: DMA mode disabled for transmission<br/>1: DMA mode enabled for transmission</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 13</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="73.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 12</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">ANFOFF:</span> Analog noise filter OFF</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Analog noise filter enabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Analog noise filter disabled</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit can be programmed only when the <span data-keyref="~i2c2_IPACRONYM">I2C</span> peripheral is disabled (PE = 0).</p></td></tr><tr height="150.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 11:8</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">DNF[3:0]</span>: Digital noise filter</p><p class="TableIndent">These bits are used to configure the digital noise filter on SDA and SCL input. The digital filter, filters spikes with a length of up to DNF[3:0]<span style="font-weight:Bold;"> * </span>t<sub>I2CCLK</sub></p><p class="TableIndent">0000: Digital filter disabled </p><p class="TableIndent">0001: Digital filter enabled and filtering capability up to one t<sub>I2CCLK</sub></p><p class="TableIndent">...</p><p class="TableIndent">1111: digital filter enabled and filtering capability up to fifteen t<sub>I2CCLK</sub></p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>If the analog filter is enabled, the digital filter is added to it. This filter can be programmed only when the <span data-keyref="~i2c2_IPACRONYM">I2C</span> peripheral is disabled (PE = 0).</p></td></tr><tr height="176.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 7</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">ERRIE</span>: Error interrupts enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Error detection interrupts disabled<br/>1: Error detection interrupts enabled</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>Any of these errors generates an interrupt:</p><p class="RegTNote_">- arbitration loss (ARLO)</p><p class="RegTNote_">- bus error detection (BERR)</p><p class="RegTNote_">- overrun/underrun (OVR)</p><p class="RegTNote_"><span data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus">- timeout detection (TIMEOUT)</span></p><p class="RegTNote_"><span data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus">- PEC error detection (PECERR)</span></p><p class="RegTNote_"><span class="Italic" data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">- alert</span><span data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus"> pin event detection (ALERT)</span></p></td></tr><tr height="106.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 6</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TCIE</span>: Transfer complete interrupt enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Transfer complete interrupt disabled<br/>1: Transfer complete interrupt enabled</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>Any of these events generates an interrupt:</p><p class="RegTNote_">Transfer complete (TC)</p><p class="RegTNote_">Transfer complete reload (TCR)</p></td></tr><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 5</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">STOPIE</span>: STOP detection interrupt enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: STOP detection (STOPF) interrupt disabled<br/>1: STOP detection (STOPF) interrupt enabled</p></td></tr><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 4</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">NACKIE</span>: Not acknowledge received interrupt enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Not acknowledge (NACKF) received interrupts disabled<br/>1: Not acknowledge (NACKF) received interrupts enabled</p></td></tr><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ADDRIE</span>: Address match interrupt enable (target only)</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Address match (ADDR) interrupts disabled<br/>1: Address match (ADDR) interrupts enabled</p></td></tr><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 2</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RXIE</span>: RX interrupt enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Receive (RXNE) interrupt disabled<br/>1: Receive (RXNE) interrupt enabled</p></td></tr><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TXIE</span>: TX interrupt enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Transmit (TXIS) interrupt disabled<br/>1: Transmit (TXIS) interrupt enabled</p></td></tr><tr height="101.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">PE</span>: Peripheral enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Peripheral disabled<br/>1: Peripheral enabled</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>When PE = 0, the <span data-keyref="~i2c2_IPACRONYM">I2C</span> SCL and SDA lines are released. Internal state machines and status bits are put back to their reset value. When cleared, PE must be kept low for at least three APB clock cycles.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 39.9.2 I2C control register 2 (I2C\_CR2)

Address offset: 0x04

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

Access: no wait states, except if a write access occurs while a write access is ongoing. In this case, wait states are inserted in the second write access until the previous one is completed. The latency of the second write access can be up to 2 x PCLK1 + 6 x I2CCLK.

style="border-left-width:0.5pt;border-left-style:solid;border-left-color:#000000;border-bottom-width:0.5pt;border-bottom-style:solid;border-bottom-color:#000000;border-right-width:0.5pt;border-right-style:solid;border-right-color:#000000;border-top-width:0.5pt;border-top-style:solid;border-top-color:#000000;"><p class="RegTBodyC"><span class="Arial7" style="background-color:transparent;">rw</span></p></td><td style="border-left-width:0.5pt;border-left-style:solid;border-left-color:#000000;border-bottom-width:0.5pt;border-bottom-style:solid;border-bottom-color:#000000;border-right-width:0.5pt;border-right-style:solid;border-right-color:#000000;border-top-width:0.5pt;border-top-style:solid;border-top-color:#000000;"><p class="RegTBodyC"><span class="Arial7" style="background-color:transparent;">rw</span></p></td><td style="border-left-width:0.5pt;border-left-style:solid;border-left-color:#000000;border-bottom-width:0.5pt;border-bottom-style:solid;border-bottom-color:#000000;border-right-width:0.5pt;border-right-style:solid;border-right-color:#000000;border-top-width:0.5pt;border-top-style:solid;border-top-color:#000000;"><p class="RegTBodyC"><span class="Arial7" style="background-color:transparent;">rw</span></p></td></tr></table></div>


 


<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="114.41px"/><col width="499.77px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:2<span data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus">7</span></p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus" height="121.33px"><td data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 26</p></td><td data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">PECBYTE</span>:<span style="font-style:Italic;"> </span>Packet error checking byte</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by software, and cleared by hardware when the PEC is transferred, or when a STOP condition or an Address matched is received, also when PE = 0.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No PEC transfer</p><p class="TableIndent">1: PEC transmission/reception is requested</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>Writing 0 to this bit has no effect.</p><p class="RegTNote_">This bit has no effect when RELOAD is set, and in target mode when SBC = 0.</p></td></tr><tr height="104.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 25</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">AUTOEND</span>:<span style="font-style:Italic;"> </span>Automatic end mode (controller mode)</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: software end mode: TC flag is set when NBYTES data are transferred, stretching SCL low.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Automatic end mode: a STOP condition is automatically sent when NBYTES data are transferred.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit has no effect in target mode or when the RELOAD bit is set.</p></td></tr><tr height="86.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 24</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RELOAD</span>:<span style="font-style:Italic;"> </span>NBYTES<span style="font-style:Italic;"> </span>reload mode</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: The transfer is completed after the NBYTES data transfer (STOP or RESTART follows).</p><p class="TableIndent">1: The transfer is not completed after the NBYTES data transfer (NBYTES is reloaded). TCR flag is set when NBYTES data are transferred, stretching SCL low.</p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 23:16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">NBYTES[7:0]</span>:<span style="font-style:Italic;"> </span>Number of bytes</p><p class="TableIndent">The number of bytes to be transmitted/received is programmed there. This field is don’t care in target mode with SBC = 0.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>Changing these bits when the START bit is set is not allowed.</p></td></tr><tr height="214.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 15</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">NACK</span>:<span style="font-style:Italic;"> </span>NACK generation (target mode)</p><p class="TableIndent">The bit is set by software, cleared by hardware when the NACK is sent, or when a STOP condition or an Address matched is received, or when PE = 0.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: an ACK is sent after current received byte.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: a NACK is sent after current received byte.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>Writing 0 to this bit has no effect.</p><p class="RegTNote_">This bit is used only in target mode: in controller receiver mode, NACK is automatically generated after last byte preceding STOP or RESTART condition, whatever the NACK bit value.</p><p class="RegTNote_">When an overrun occurs in target receiver NOSTRETCH mode, a NACK is automatically generated, whatever the NACK bit value.</p><p class="RegTNote_"><span data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus">When hardware PEC checking is enabled (PECBYTE = 1), the PEC acknowledge value does not depend on the NACK value.</span></p></td></tr><tr height="104.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 14</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">STOP</span>:<span style="font-style:Italic;"> </span>STOP condition generation</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit only pertains to controller mode. It is set by software and cleared by hardware when a STOP condition is detected or when PE = 0.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No STOP generation</p><p class="TableIndent">1: STOP generation after current byte transfer</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>Writing 0 to this bit has no effect.</p></td></tr><tr height="214.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 13</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">START</span>: START condition generation</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by software. It is cleared by hardware after the START condition followed by the address sequence is sent, by an arbitration loss, by a timeout error detection, or when PE = 0.<span data-conditional="_noi2c2_v14"> It can also be cleared by software, by setting the ADDRCF bit of the <span data-keyref="~i2c2_IPACRONYM">I2C</span>_ICR register.</span></p><p class="TableIndent">0: No START generation</p><p class="TableIndent">1: RESTART/START generation:</p><p class="TableIndent">If the <span data-keyref="~i2c2_IPACRONYM">I2C</span> is already in controller mode with AUTOEND = 0, setting this bit generates a repeated START condition when RELOAD = 0, after the end of the NBYTES transfer.</p><p class="TableIndent">Otherwise, setting this bit generates a START condition once the bus is free.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>Writing 0 to this bit has no effect.</p><p class="RegTNote_">The START bit can be set even if the bus is BUSY or <span data-keyref="~i2c2_IPACRONYM">I2C</span> is in target mode.</p><p class="RegTNote_">This bit has no effect when RELOAD is set. </p></td></tr><tr height="117.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 12</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">HEAD10R</span>:<span style="font-style:Italic;"> </span>10-bit address header only read direction (controller receiver mode)</p><p class="TableIndent">0: The controller sends the complete 10-bit target address read sequence: START + 2 bytes 10-bit address in write direction + RESTART + first seven bits of the 10-bit address in read direction.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: The controller sends only the first seven bits of the 10-bit address, followed by read direction.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>Changing this bit when the START bit is set is not allowed.</p></td></tr><tr height="73.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 11</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ADD10</span>:<span style="font-style:Italic;"> </span>10-bit addressing mode (controller mode)</p><p class="TableIndent">0: The controller operates in 7-bit addressing mode</p><p class="TableIndent">1: The controller operates in 10-bit addressing mode</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>Changing this bit when the START bit is set is not allowed.</p></td></tr><tr height="73.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 10</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RD_WRN</span>: Transfer direction (controller mode)</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Controller requests a write transfer</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Controller requests a read transfer</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>Changing this bit when the START bit is set is not allowed.</p></td></tr><tr height="120.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 9:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">SADD[9:0]</span>: Target address (controller mode)</p><p class="TableIndent"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">Condition: In 7-bit addressing mode (ADD10 = 0)</span>:</p><p class="TableIndent">SADD[7:1] must be written with the 7-bit target address to be sent. Bits SADD[9], SADD[8] and SADD[0] are don't care.</p><p class="TableIndent"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">Condition: In 10-bit addressing mode (ADD10 = 1)</span>:</p><p class="TableIndent">SADD[9:0] must be written with the 10-bit target address to be sent.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>Changing these bits when the START bit is set is not allowed.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 39.9.3 I2C own address 1 register (I2C\_OAR1)

Address offset: 0x08

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

Access: no wait states, except if a write access occurs while a write access is ongoing. In this case, wait states are inserted in the second write access until the previous one is completed. The latency of the second write access can be up to 2 x PCLK1 + 6 x I2CCLK.

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="114.39px"/><col width="499.79px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 15</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">OA1EN</span>: Own address 1 enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Own address 1 disabled. The received target address OA1 is NACKed.<br/>1: Own address 1 enabled. The received target address OA1 is ACKed.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 14:11</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="73.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 10</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">OA1MODE</span>: Own address 1 10-bit mode</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Own address 1 is a 7-bit address.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Own address 1 is a 10-bit address.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit can be written only when OA1EN = 0.</p></td></tr><tr height="88.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 9:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">OA1[9:0]</span>: Interface own target address</p><p class="TableIndent">7-bit addressing mode: OA1[7:1] contains the 7-bit own target address. Bits OA1[9], OA1[8] and OA1[0] are don't care.</p><p class="TableIndent">10-bit addressing mode: OA1[9:0] contains the 10-bit own target address.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>These bits can be written only when OA1EN = 0.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 39.9.4 I2C own address 2 register (I2C\_OAR2)

Address offset: 0x0C

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

Access: no wait states, except if a write access occurs while a write access is ongoing. In this case, wait states are inserted in the second write access, until the previous one is completed. The latency of the second write access can be up to 2x PCLK1 + 6 x I2CCLK.

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="114.39px"/><col width="499.79px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="56.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 15</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">OA2EN</span>: Own address 2 enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Own address 2 disabled. The received target address OA2 is NACKed.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Own address 2 enabled. The received target address OA2 is ACKed.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 14:11</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="216.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 10:8</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">OA2MSK[2:0]</span>: Own address 2 masks</p><p class="TableIndent">000: No mask</p><p class="TableIndent">001: OA2[1] is masked and don’t care. Only OA2[7:2] are compared.</p><p class="TableIndent">010: OA2[2:1] are masked and don’t care. Only OA2[7:3] are compared.</p><p class="TableIndent">011: OA2[3:1] are masked and don’t care. Only OA2[7:4] are compared.</p><p class="TableIndent">100: OA2[4:1] are masked and don’t care. Only OA2[7:5] are compared.</p><p class="TableIndent">101: OA2[5:1] are masked and don’t care. Only OA2[7:6] are compared.</p><p class="TableIndent">110: OA2[6:1] are masked and don’t care. Only OA2[7] is compared.</p><p class="TableIndent">111: OA2[7:1] are masked and don’t care. No comparison is done, and all (except reserved) 7-bit received addresses are acknowledged.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>These bits can be written only when OA2EN = 0.</p><p class="RegTNote_">As soon as OA2MSK ≠ 0, the reserved <span data-keyref="~i2c2_IPACRONYM">I2C</span> addresses (0b0000xxx and 0b1111xxx) are not acknowledged, even if the comparison matches.</p></td></tr><tr height="57.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 7:1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">OA2[7:1]</span>: Interface address</p><p class="TableIndent">7-bit addressing mode: 7-bit address</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>These bits can be written only when OA2EN = 0.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 39.9.5 I2C timing register (I2C\_TIMINGR)

Address offset: 0x10

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

Access: no wait states

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="114.39px"/><col width="499.79px"/><tr height="85.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:28</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">PRESC[3:0]</span>: Timing prescaler</p><p class="TableIndent">This field is used to prescale <span data-keyref="~i2c2_ker-ck-Name">I2CCLK</span> to generate the clock period t<sub>PRESC </sub>used for data setup and hold counters (refer to section <a href="#XREF_27311__I2C_timings32" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">I2C timings</a>), and for SCL high and low level counters (refer to section <a href="#XREF_42919__I2C_master_initialization32" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">I2C controller initialization</a>).</p><p class="TableIndent">t<sub>PRESC </sub>= (PRESC + 1) x t<sub>I2CCLK</sub></p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 27:24</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="86.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 23:20</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">SCLDEL[3:0]</span>: Data setup time</p><p class="TableIndent">This field is used to generate a delay t<sub>SCLDEL</sub> = (SCLDEL + 1) x t<sub>PRESC</sub> between SDA edge and SCL rising edge. In controller and in target modes with NOSTRETCH = 0, the SCL line is stretched low during t<sub>SCLDEL</sub>.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>t<sub><span class="Subscript" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">SCLDEL</span></sub> is used to generate t<sub>SU:DAT </sub>timing.</p></td></tr><tr height="102.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 19:16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">SDADEL[3:0]</span>: Data hold time</p><p class="TableIndent">This field is used to generate the delay t<sub>SDADEL </sub>between SCL falling edge and SDA edge. In controller and in target modes with NOSTRETCH = 0, the SCL line is stretched low during t<sub>SDADEL</sub>.</p><p class="TableIndent">t<sub>SDADEL</sub>= SDADEL x t<sub>PRESC</sub></p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>SDADEL is used to generate t<sub>HD:DAT </sub>timing.</p></td></tr><tr height="73.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 15:8</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">SCLH[7:0]</span>: SCL high period (controller mode)</p><p class="TableIndent">This field is used to generate the SCL high period in controller mode.</p><p class="TableIndent">t<sub>SCLH </sub>= (SCLH + 1) x t<sub>PRESC</sub></p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>SCLH is also used to generate t<sub>SU:STO </sub>and t<sub>HD:STA</sub><sub><span style="font-style:normal;"> </span></sub>timing.</p></td></tr><tr height="73.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 7:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">SCLL[7:0]</span>: SCL low period (controller mode)</p><p class="TableIndent">This field is used to generate the SCL low period in controller mode.</p><p class="TableIndent">t<sub>SCLL </sub>= (SCLL + 1) x t<sub>PRESC</sub></p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>SCLL is also used to generate t<sub>BUF </sub>and t<sub>SU:STA</sub><sub><span style="font-style:normal;"> </span></sub>timings.</p></td></tr></table></div>


**Note:**

This register must be configured when the I2C peripheral is disabled (PE = 0).

**Note:**

The STM32CubeMX tool calculates and provides theI2C\_TIMINGR content in the I2C Configuration window.

### 39.9.6 I2C timeout register (I2C\_TIMEOUTR)![](RM0440_STM32G4xx_c7amba_i2c2_UserSpec_FOM_SVG_1240.svg)

Address offset: 0x14

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

Access: no wait states, except if a write access occurs while a write access is ongoing. In this case, wait states are inserted in the second write access until the previous one is completed. The latency of the second write access can be up to 2 x PCLK1 + 6 x I2CCLK.

<div align="left" data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="114.39px"/><col width="499.79px"/><tr height="70.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 31</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TEXTEN</span>: Extended clock timeout enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Extended clock timeout detection is disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Extended clock timeout detection is enabled. When a cumulative SCL stretch for more than t<sub>LOW:EXT </sub>is <span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">done by the <span data-keyref="~i2c2_IPACRONYM">I2C</span> interface</span>, a timeout error is detected (TIMEOUT = 1).</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 30:28</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="122.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 27:16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TIMEOUTB[11:0]</span>: Bus timeout B</p><p class="TableIndent">This field is used to configure the cumulative clock extension timeout:</p><p class="RegTBullet2">–<span style="padding-right:16pt ;">​</span>Controller mode: the controller cumulative clock low extend time (t<sub><span class="Subscript" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">LOW:MEXT</span></sub>) is detected</p><p class="RegTBullet2">–<span style="padding-right:16pt ;">​</span>Target mode: the target cumulative clock low extend time (t<sub><span class="Subscript" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">LOW:SEXT</span></sub>) is detected</p><p class="TableIndent">t<sub>LOW:EXT </sub>= (TIMEOUTB + TIDLE = 01) x 2048 x t<sub>I2CCLK</sub></p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>These bits can be written only when TEXTEN = 0.</p></td></tr><tr height="70.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 15</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TIMOUTEN</span>: Clock timeout enable </p><p class="TableIndent">0: SCL timeout detection is disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: SCL timeout detection is enabled. When SCL is low for more than t<sub>TIMEOUT</sub> (TIDLE = 0) or high for more than t<sub>IDLE </sub>(TIDLE = 1), a timeout error is detected (TIMEOUT = 1).</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 14:13</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="73.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 12</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TIDLE</span>: Idle clock timeout detection</p><p class="TableIndent">0: TIMEOUTA is used to detect SCL low timeout</p><p class="TableIndent">1: TIMEOUTA is used to detect both SCL and SDA high timeout (bus idle condition)</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit can be written only when TIMOUTEN = 0.</p></td></tr><tr height="121.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 11:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TIMEOUTA[11:0]</span>: Bus timeout A</p><p class="TableIndent">This field is used to configure:</p><p class="TableIndent">The SCL low timeout condition t<sub>TIMEOUT</sub> when TIDLE = 0</p><p class="TableIndent">t<sub>TIMEOUT</sub>= (TIMEOUTA + 1) x 2048 x t<sub>I2CCLK</sub></p><p class="TableIndent">The bus idle condition (both SCL and SDA high) when TIDLE = 1</p><p class="TableIndent">t<sub>IDLE</sub>= (TIMEOUTA + 1) x 4 x t<sub>I2CCLK</sub></p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>These bits can be written only when TIMOUTEN = 0.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 39.9.7 I2C interrupt and status register (I2C\_ISR)

Address offset: 0x18

Reset value: 0x0000 0001

Access: no wait states

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="116.15px"/><col width="498.02px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:24</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="69.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 23:17</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ADDCODE[6:0]</span>: Address match code (target mode) </p><p class="TableIndent">These bits are updated with the received address when an address match event occurs (ADDR = 1). In the case of a 10-bit address, ADDCODE provides the 10-bit header followed by the two MSBs of the address.</p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">DIR</span>:<span style="font-style:Italic;"> </span>Transfer direction (target mode)</p><p class="TableIndent">This flag is updated when an address match event occurs (ADDR = 1).</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Write transfer, target enters receiver mode.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Read transfer, target enters transmitter mode.</p></td></tr><tr height="69.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 15</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">BUSY</span>:<span style="font-style:Italic;"> </span>Bus busy</p><p class="TableIndent">This flag indicates that a communication is in progress on the bus. It is set by hardware when a START condition is detected, and cleared by hardware when a STOP condition is detected, or when PE = 0.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 14</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus" height="86.67px"><td data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 13</p></td><td data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ALERT</span>: SMBus alert</p><p class="TableIndent">This flag is set by hardware when SMBHEN = 1 (SMBus host configuration), ALERTEN = 1 and an SMBALERT# event (falling edge) is detected on SMBA pin. It is cleared by software by setting the ALERTCF bit.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit is cleared by hardware when PE = 0.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus" height="72.00px"><td data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 12</p></td><td data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TIMEOUT</span>: Timeout or t<sub><span class="Subscript" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">LOW</span></sub> detection flag</p><p class="TableIndent">This flag is set by hardware when a timeout or extended clock timeout occurred. It is cleared by software by setting the TIMEOUTCF bit.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit is cleared by hardware when PE = 0.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus" height="86.67px"><td data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 11</p></td><td data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">PECERR</span>: PEC error in reception</p><p class="TableIndent">This flag is set by hardware when the received PEC does not match with the PEC register content. A NACK is automatically sent after the wrong PEC reception. It is cleared by software by setting the PECCF bit.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit is cleared by hardware when PE = 0.</p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 10</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">OVR</span>:<span style="font-weight:Bold;"> </span>Overrun/underrun (target mode)</p><p class="TableIndent">This flag is set by hardware in target mode with NOSTRETCH = 1, when an overrun/underrun error occurs. It is cleared by software by setting the OVRCF bit<span style="font-style:Italic;">.</span></p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit is cleared by hardware when PE = 0.</p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 9</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ARLO</span>:<span style="font-style:Italic;"> </span>Arbitration lost</p><p class="TableIndent">This flag is set by hardware in case of arbitration loss. It is cleared by software by setting the ARLOCF bit.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit is cleared by hardware when PE = 0.</p></td></tr><tr height="86.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 8</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">BERR</span>: Bus error</p><p class="TableIndent">This flag is set by hardware when a misplaced START or STOP condition is detected whereas the peripheral is involved in the transfer. The flag is not set during the address phase in target mode. It is cleared by software by setting the BERRCF bit.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit is cleared by hardware when PE = 0. </p></td></tr><tr height="89.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 7</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TCR</span>:<span style="font-weight:Bold;"> </span>Transfer complete reload</p><p class="TableIndent">This flag is set by hardware when RELOAD = 1 and NBYTES data have been transferred. It is cleared by software when NBYTES is written to a non-zero value<span style="font-style:Italic;">.</span></p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit is cleared by hardware when PE = 0.</p><p class="RegTNote_">This flag is only for controller mode, or for target mode when the SBC bit is set.</p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 6</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TC</span>:<span style="font-weight:Bold;"> </span>Transfer complete (controller mode)</p><p class="TableIndent">This flag is set by hardware when RELOAD = 0, AUTOEND = 0 and NBYTES data have been transferred. It is cleared by software when START bit or STOP bit is set<span style="font-style:Italic;">.</span></p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit is cleared by hardware when PE = 0.</p></td></tr><tr height="138.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 5</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">STOPF</span>: STOP detection flag</p><p class="TableIndent">This flag is set by hardware when a STOP condition is detected on the bus and the peripheral is involved in this transfer:</p><p class="RegTBullet2">–<span style="padding-right:16pt ;">​</span>as a controller, provided that the STOP condition is generated by the peripheral.</p><p class="RegTBullet2">–<span style="padding-right:16pt ;">​</span>as a target, provided that the peripheral has been addressed previously during this transfer.</p><p class="TableBodyL">It is cleared by software by setting the STOPCF bit.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit is cleared by hardware when PE = 0. </p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 4</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">NACKF</span>:<span style="font-weight:Bold;"> </span>Not acknowledge received flag</p><p class="TableIndent">This flag is set by hardware when a NACK is received after a byte transmission. It is cleared by software by setting the NACKCF bit.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit is cleared by hardware when PE = 0.</p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ADDR</span>:<span style="font-style:Italic;"> </span>Address matched (target mode)</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by hardware as soon as the received target address matched with one of the enabled target addresses. It is cleared by software by setting <span style="font-style:Italic;">ADDRCF bit.</span></p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit is cleared by hardware when PE = 0. </p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 2</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RXNE</span>: Receive data register not empty (receivers)</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by hardware when the received data is copied into the <span data-keyref="~i2c2_IPACRONYM">I2C</span>_RXDR register, and is ready to be read. It is cleared when <span data-keyref="~i2c2_IPACRONYM">I2C</span>_RXDR is read.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit is cleared by hardware when PE = 0.</p></td></tr><tr height="117.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TXIS</span>: Transmit interrupt status (transmitters)</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by hardware when the <span data-keyref="~i2c2_IPACRONYM">I2C</span>_TXDR register is empty and the data to be transmitted must be written in the <span data-keyref="~i2c2_IPACRONYM">I2C</span>_TXDR register. It is cleared when the next data to be sent is written in the <span data-keyref="~i2c2_IPACRONYM">I2C</span>_TXDR register.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit can be written to 1 by software only when NOSTRETCH = 1, to generate a TXIS event (interrupt if TXIE = 1 or DMA request if TXDMAEN = 1).</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit is cleared by hardware when PE = 0.</p></td></tr><tr height="88.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TXE</span>: Transmit data register empty (transmitters)</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by hardware when the <span data-keyref="~i2c2_IPACRONYM">I2C</span>_TXDR register is empty. It is cleared when the next data to be sent is written in the <span data-keyref="~i2c2_IPACRONYM">I2C</span>_TXDR register. </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit can be written to 1 by software in order to flush the transmit data register <span data-keyref="~i2c2_IPACRONYM">I2C</span>_TXDR.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit is set by hardware when PE = 0.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 39.9.8 I2C interrupt clear register (I2C\_ICR)

Address offset: 0x1C

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

Access: no wait states

data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 13</p></td><td data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ALERTCF</span>: Alert flag clear</p><p class="TableIndent">Writing 1 to this bit clears the ALERT flag in the <span data-keyref="~i2c2_IPACRONYM">I2C</span>_ISR register.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus" height="40.00px"><td data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 12</p></td><td data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TIMOUTCF</span>: Timeout detection flag clear</p><p class="TableIndent">Writing 1 to this bit clears the TIMEOUT flag in the <span data-keyref="~i2c2_IPACRONYM">I2C</span>_ISR register.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus" height="40.00px"><td data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 11</p></td><td data-conditional="_i2c2_smbus"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">PECCF</span>: PEC error flag clear</p><p class="TableIndent">Writing 1 to this bit clears the PECERR flag in the <span data-keyref="~i2c2_IPACRONYM">I2C</span>_ISR register.</p></td></tr><tr height="40.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 10</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">OVRCF</span>:<span style="font-weight:Bold;"> </span>Overrun/underrun flag clear</p><p class="TableIndent">Writing 1 to this bit clears the OVR flag in the <span data-keyref="~i2c2_IPACRONYM">I2C</span>_ISR register.</p></td></tr><tr height="40.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 9</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ARLOCF</span>:<span style="font-style:Italic;"> </span>Arbitration lost flag clear</p><p class="TableIndent">Writing 1 to this bit clears the ARLO flag in the <span data-keyref="~i2c2_IPACRONYM">I2C</span>_ISR register.</p></td></tr><tr height="40.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 8</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">BERRCF</span>: Bus error flag clear</p><p class="TableIndent">Writing 1 to this bit clears the BERRF flag in the <span data-keyref="~i2c2_IPACRONYM">I2C</span>_ISR register.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 7:6</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="40.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 5</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">STOPCF</span>: STOP detection flag clear</p><p class="TableIndent">Writing 1 to this bit clears the STOPF flag in the <span data-keyref="~i2c2_IPACRONYM">I2C</span>_ISR register.</p></td></tr><tr height="40.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 4</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">NACKCF</span>:<span style="font-weight:Bold;"> </span>Not acknowledge flag clear</p><p class="TableIndent">Writing 1 to this bit clears the NACKF flag in <span data-keyref="~i2c2_IPACRONYM">I2C</span>_ISR register.</p></td></tr><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ADDRCF</span>:<span style="font-style:Italic;"> </span>Address matched flag clear </p><p class="TableIndent">Writing 1 to this bit clears the ADDR flag in the <span data-keyref="~i2c2_IPACRONYM">I2C</span>_ISR register. Writing 1 to this bit also clears the START bit in the <span data-keyref="~i2c2_IPACRONYM">I2C</span>_CR2 register.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 2:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 39.9.9 I2C PEC register (I2C\_PECR)![](RM0440_STM32G4xx_c7amba_i2c2_UserSpec_FOM_SVG_1241.svg)

Address offset: 0x20

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

Access: no wait states

### 39.9.10 I2C receive data register (I2C\_RXDR)

Address offset: 0x24

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

Access: no wait states

### 39.9.11 I2C transmit data register (I2C\_TXDR)

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

Access: no wait states
